      |         ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1742 |           // cur = (k0/16) & 1. The staging writes the OTHER buffer, so the two
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1743 |           // never alias within a step and ONE barrier per iteration suffices:
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1744 |           // reads of a buffer in step k-1 precede that barrier, writes of the
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1745 |           // same buffer in step k follow it.
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1746 |           Value step = kb.create<arith::DivUIOp>(l, k0, c16);
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1747 |           Value cur = kb.create<arith::RemUIOp>(l, step, ci(2));
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1748 |           Value nxt = kb.create<arith::SubIOp>(l, ci(1), cur);
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1749 |           auto sel = [&](Value which, Value unit) {
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1750 |             return Value(kb.create<arith::MulIOp>(l, which, unit));
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1751 |           };
      |           ~~                     
 1752 |           // ISSUE: slab k0+16 into `nxt`. Past the end its loads mask to zero
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1753 |           // and nothing ever reads the result, so no guard is needed.
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1754 |           Staged st;
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~             
 1755 |           emitStage(kb, l, kb.create<arith::AddIOp>(l, k0, c16),
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1756 |                     sel(nxt, cAOff), sel(nxt, cBOff), &st);
      |                     ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1757 |           // COMPUTE on `cur` while those loads are outstanding.
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1758 |           SmallVector<Value> next;
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1759 |           emitCompute(kb, l, accs, sel(cur, cWgMv), sel(cur, cWgNv), next);
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1760 |           // DRAIN: only now does anything wait on the loads.
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1761 |           emitDrain(kb, l, st);
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~  
 1762 |           // Describe the pipeline: issue the loads, then alternate staging
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1763 |           // VALU with single matrix ops so the U pipe is busy through the
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1764 |           // chain, then drain to LDS. The independence this relies on is
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1765 |           // exactly what double-buffering bought.
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1766 |           if (ldsSchedValuPerMma != 0) {
      |           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1767 |             auto i32 = kb.getI32Type();
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

      |                   ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

 1772 |             };

 1773 |             grp(ROCDL::SchedGroupMask::vmem_read, depthA + depthB);
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1775 |             // alternation. It exists because the alternation measurably did not
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1776 |             // happen -- the ISA shows zero VALU between the first and last wmma
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1777 |             // at every positive N -- while the option still helped. Without
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1778 |             // this arm the gain would be credited to a mechanism that is not
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1779 |             // running.
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~          
 1780 |             // N == -2 is the MECHANISM arm: the matrix groups WITHOUT any
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1781 |             // valu groups. The schedule diff says the alternation's VALU half
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1782 |             // places nothing (zero VALU in the chain at every N) while its
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1783 |             // mfma half clusters the matrix ops ahead of the ds_write group,
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1784 |             // which is what lifts the global-load waits out of the chain. If
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1785 |             // that reading is right this arm reproduces the whole gain.
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1786 |             if (ldsSchedValuPerMma > 0)
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1787 |               for (int64_t g = 0; g < mt * nt; ++g) {

 1793 |                 grp(ROCDL::SchedGroupMask::mfma_wmma, 1);

 1794 |             // N == -3 discriminates the remaining reading. Measured: the mfma

 1795 |             // groups ALONE are worse than no description at all (0.973x), so
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1796 |             // the valu groups are load-bearing even though they place no VALU
      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
 1797 |             // in the chain -- they act as a RESERVATION that keeps the

      |             ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
